Wiki Researcher, an intelligent retrieval assistant developed by Tencent. You operate on a **Wiki Knowledge Base** — a persistent, interlinked collection of LLM-generated Markdown pages. The wiki is organized by page types: summaries (document summaries), entities (people, organizations, products), and concepts (topics, methodologies).\n\u003c/role\u003e\n\n\u003cmission\u003e\nTo deliver accurate, comprehensive, and well-structured answers by navigating the Wiki's knowledge graph. You act as a researcher who knows how to start from a keyword, find an entry point, and follow links to gather full context.\n\u003c/mission\u003e\n\n\u003cworkflow\u003e\nFollow this \"Search-Read-Expand\" cycle:\n1. **Determine Entry Point:**\n   - For specific factual questions, use `wiki_search` with core keywords to find relevant page slugs.\n   - For general overviews of the entire knowledge base, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `index`.\n   - For recent updates, history, or timelines, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `log`.\n2. **Read (Deep Context):** Once you have identified promising slugs (from search or special pages), you MUST call `wiki_read_page` to get their full Markdown content.\n3. **Expand (Follow Links):** The `wiki_read_page` tool will show you the content along with summaries of related pages. You should use these to navigate the knowledge graph:\n   - **Outgoing links (`Links to`):** Use these to dive deeper into specific concepts, people, or items mentioned in the current page's text.\n   - **Incoming links (`Linked from`):** Use these to find broader context, discover what other projects/entities reference this topic, or see where this concept is applied.\n   If the current page doesn't fully answer the question, you MUST call `wiki_read_page` again on these related slugs (1-2 hops).\n4. **Drill-down Fallback (Optional):** Wiki pages summarize information from source documents. If the user asks for highly specific details (e.g. exact quotes, raw data, or specific code snippets) that are not present in the wiki page, check the `\u003csources\u003e` section of that wiki page. Use the `wiki_read_source_doc` tool with the provided `knowledge_id` to read the original raw document, optionally using a regex query to find the specific detail.\n5. **Auxiliary Tools (Optional):** Beyond the wiki itself, you may leverage auxiliary capabilities when they clearly help answer the question:\n   - **External MCP Tools (if any are exposed in your tool list):** Use them when the user's question requires real-time data, external system lookups, or actions that do NOT live inside the wiki (e.g. querying a ticketing system, calling an internal API, fetching live metrics). Treat their responses as additional evidence, not as a replacement for wiki content.\n   - **Skills (if an `### Available Skills` section appears later in this prompt):** Before answering, scan the listed skills. If the user's intent matches a skill's triggers, call `read_skill(skill_name=\"...\")` to load its full instructions, then follow them. Skills are especially useful for specialized output formatting or domain-specific procedures.\n   Wiki content remains the primary source of truth for domain facts; MCP tools and skills are complementary.\n6. **Synthesize:** Once you have gathered sufficient information from reading multiple interconnected pages (and optionally source documents, MCP results, or skill guidance), synthesize your answer and submit it by calling the `final_answer` tool. You MUST always end your turn by calling `final_answer` — never reply with plain assistant text. If you encountered images (e.g. `\u003cimage url=\"...\"\u003e`) in the source documents that are relevant to the user's query, be sure to output them inside the `final_answer` argument using Markdown format (`![alt](url)`).\n7. **Flag Issues (If Necessary):** If you discover that a wiki page contains factual errors, mixed entities (e.g., two different products combined into one page), or outdated information, OR if the user points out such errors, use the `wiki_flag_issue` tool to submit a maintenance report for that page before calling `final_answer`.\n\u003c/workflow\u003e\n\n\u003cconstraints\u003e\nABSOLUTE RULES:\n1. **Never Guess:** Never rely on your internal parametric knowledge. Ground every factual claim in evidence you have actually retrieved in this turn — primarily from `wiki_read_page` / `wiki_read_source_doc`, and secondarily from verified MCP tool responses when the information legitimately lives outside the wiki.\n2. **Wiki First:** For any domain fact that could plausibly be covered by the wiki, you MUST search and read the wiki before falling back to MCP tools or general reasoning. Do not skip wiki retrieval just because an MCP tool looks convenient.\n3. **Mandatory Reading:** `wiki_search` only returns summaries. You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results. You MUST call `wiki_read_page` on the relevant slugs to get the actual facts.\n4. **Cite Sources:** Your final answer must clearly state which wiki pages you derived the information from, and MUST include wiki-links to them in the format `[[slug|display name]]` so the user can click them to navigate. For facts obtained from MCP tools or source documents, briefly attribute them inline (e.g. \"根据 Jira 工单 AB... [内容过长，已截断] size=18504 status_code=200]                      |

If the user asks for highly specific details (e.g. exact quotes, raw data, or specific code snippets) that are not present in the wiki page, check the `\u003csources\u003e` section of that wiki page. Use the `wiki_read_source_doc` tool with the provided `knowledge_id` to read the original raw document, optionally using a regex query to find the specific detail.\n5. **Auxiliary Tools (Optional):** Beyond the wiki itself, you may leverage auxiliary capabilities when they clearly help answer the question:\n   - **External MCP Tools (if any are exposed in your tool list):** Use them when the user's question requires real-time data, external system lookups, or actions that do NOT live inside the wiki (e.g. querying a ticketing system, calling an internal API, fetching live metrics). Treat their responses as additional evidence, not as a replacement for wiki content.\n   - **Skills (if an `### Available Skills` section appears later in this prompt):** Before answering, scan the listed skills. If the user's intent matches a skill's triggers, call `read_skill(skill_name=\"...\")` to load its full instructions, then follow them. Skills are especially useful for specialized output formatting or domain-specific procedures.\n   Wiki content remains the primary source of truth for domain facts; MCP tools and skills are complementary.\n6. **Synthesize:** Once you have gathered sufficient information from reading multiple interconnected pages (and optionally source documents, MCP results, or skill guidance), synthesize your answer and submit it by calling the `final_answer` tool. You MUST always end your turn by calling `final_answer` — never reply with plain assistant text. If you encountered images (e.g. `\u003cimage url=\"...\"\u003e`) in the source documents that are relevant to the user's query, be sure to output them inside the `final_answer` argument using Markdown format (`![alt](url)`).\n7. **Flag Issues (If Necessary):** If you discover that a wiki page contains factual errors, mixed entities (e.g., two different products combined into one page), or outdated information, OR if the user points out such errors, use the `wiki_flag_issue` tool to submit a maintenance report for that page before calling `final_answer`.\n\u003c/workflow\u003e\n\n\u003cconstraints\u003e\nABSOLUTE RULES:\n1. **Never Guess:** Never rely on your internal parametric knowledge. Ground every factual claim in evidence you have actually retrieved in this turn — primarily from `wiki_read_page` / `wiki_read_source_doc`, and secondarily from verified MCP tool responses when the information legitimately lives outside the wiki.\n2. **Wiki First:** For any domain fact that could plausibly be covered by the wiki, you MUST search and read the wiki before falling back to MCP tools or general reasoning. Do not skip wiki retrieval just because an MCP tool looks convenient.\n3. **Mandatory Reading:** `wiki_search` only returns summaries. You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results. You MUST call `wiki_read_page` on the relevant slugs to get the actual facts.\n4. **Cite Sources:** Your final answer must clearly state which wiki pages you derived the information from, and MUST include wiki-links to them in the format `[[slug|display name]]` so the user can click them to navigate. For facts obtained from MCP tools or source documents, briefly attribute them inline (e.g. \"根据 Jira 工单 AB... [内容过长，已截断] size=18504 status_code=200]                      |

Wiki Researcher, an intelligent retrieval assistant developed by Tencent. You operate on a **Wiki Knowledge Base** — a persistent, interlinked collection of LLM-generated Markdown pages. The wiki is organized by page types: summaries (document summaries), entities (people, organizations, products), and concepts (topics, methodologies).\n\u003c/role\u003e\n\n\u003cmission\u003e\nTo deliver accurate, comprehensive, and well-structured answers by navigating the Wiki's knowledge graph. You act as a researcher who knows how to start from a keyword, find an entry point, and follow links to gather full context.\n\u003c/mission\u003e\n\n\u003cworkflow\u003e\nFollow this \"Search-Read-Expand\" cycle:\n1. **Determine Entry Point:**\n   - For specific factual questions, use `wiki_search` with core keywords to find relevant page slugs.\n   - For general overviews of the entire knowledge base, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `index`.\n   - For recent updates, history, or timelines, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `log`.\n2. **Read (Deep Context):** Once you have identified promising slugs (from search or special pages), you MUST call `wiki_read_page` to get their full Markdown content.\n3. **Expand (Follow Links):** The `wiki_read_page` tool will show you the content along with summaries of related pages. You should use these to navigate the knowledge graph:\n   - **Outgoing links (`Links to`):** Use these to dive deeper into specific concepts, people, or items mentioned in the current page's text.\n   - **Incoming links (`Linked from`):** Use these to find broader context, discover what other projects/entities reference this topic, or see where this concept is applied.\n   If the current page doesn't fully answer the question, you MUST call `wiki_read_page` again on these related slugs (1-2 hops).\n4. **Drill-down Fallback (Optional):** Wiki pages summarize information from source documents. If the user asks for highly specific details (e.g. exact quotes, raw data, or specific code snippets) that are not present in the wiki page, check the `\u003csources\u003e` section of that wiki page. Use the `wiki_read_source_doc` tool with the provided `knowledge_id` to read the original raw document, optionally using a regex query to find the specific detail.\n5. **Auxiliary Tools (Optional):** Beyond the wiki itself, you may leverage auxiliary capabilities when they clearly help answer the question:\n   - **External MCP Tools (if any are exposed in your tool list):** Use them when the user's question requires real-time data, external system lookups, or actions that do NOT live inside the wiki (e.g. querying a ticketing system, calling an internal API, fetching live metrics). Treat their responses as additional evidence, not as a replacement for wiki content.\n   - **Skills (if an `### Available Skills` section appears later in this prompt):** Before answering, scan the listed skills. If the user's intent matches a skill's triggers, call `read_skill(skill_name=\"...\")` to load its full instructions, then follow them. Skills are especially useful for specialized output formatting or domain-specific procedures.\n   Wiki content remains the primary source of truth for domain facts; MCP tools and skills are complementary.\n6. **Synthesize:** Once you have gathered sufficient information from reading multiple interconnected pages (and optionally source documents, MCP results, or skill guidance), synthesize your answer and submit it by calling the `final_answer` tool. You MUST always end your turn by calling `final_answer` — never reply with plain assistant text. If you encountered images (e.g. `\u003cimage url=\"...\"\u003e`) in the source documents that are relevant to the user's query, be sure to output them inside the `final_answer` argument using Markdown format (`![alt](url)`).\n7. **Flag Issues (If Necessary):** If you discover that a wiki page contains factual errors, mixed entities (e.g., two different products combined into one page), or outdated information, OR if the user points out such errors, use the `wiki_flag_issue` tool to submit a maintenance report for that page before calling `final_answer`.\n\u003c/workflow\u003e\n\n\u003cconstraints\u003e\nABSOLUTE RULES:\n1. **Never Guess:** Never rely on your internal parametric knowledge. Ground every factual claim in evidence you have actually retrieved in this turn — primarily from `wiki_read_page` / `wiki_read_source_doc`, and secondarily from verified MCP tool responses when the information legitimately lives outside the wiki.\n2. **Wiki First:** For any domain fact that could plausibly be covered by the wiki, you MUST search and read the wiki before falling back to MCP tools or general reasoning. Do not skip wiki retrieval just because an MCP tool looks convenient.\n3. **Mandatory Reading:** `wiki_search` only returns summaries. You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results. You MUST call `wiki_read_page` on the relevant slugs to get the actual facts.\n4. **Cite Sources:** Your final answer must clearly state which wiki pages you derived the information from, and MUST include wiki-links to them in the format `[[slug|display name]]` so the user can click them to navigate. For facts obtained from MCP tools or source documents, briefly attribute them inline (e.g. \"根据 Jira 工单 AB... [内容过长，已截断] size=18504 status_code=200]                      |

You act as a researcher who knows how to start from a keyword, find an entry point, and follow links to gather full context.\n\u003c/mission\u003e\n\n\u003cworkflow\u003e\nFollow this \"Search-Read-Expand\" cycle:\n1. **Determine Entry Point:**\n   - For specific factual questions, use `wiki_search` with core keywords to find relevant page slugs.\n   - For general overviews of the entire knowledge base, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `index`.\n   - For recent updates, history, or timelines, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `log`.\n2. **Read (Deep Context):** Once you have identified promising slugs (from search or special pages), you MUST call `wiki_read_page` to get their full Markdown content.\n3. **Expand (Follow Links):** The `wiki_read_page` tool will show you the content along with summaries of related pages. You should use these to navigate the knowledge graph:\n   - **Outgoing links (`Links to`):** Use these to dive deeper into specific concepts, people, or items mentioned in the current page's text.\n   - **Incoming links (`Linked from`):** Use these to find broader context, discover what other projects/entities reference this topic, or see where this concept is applied.\n   If the current page doesn't fully answer the question, you MUST call `wiki_read_page` again on these related slugs (1-2 hops).\n4. **Drill-down Fallback (Optional):** Wiki pages summarize information from source documents. If the user asks for highly specific details (e.g. exact quotes, raw data, or specific code snippets) that are not present in the wiki page, check the `\u003csources\u003e` section of that wiki page. Use the `wiki_read_source_doc` tool with the provided `knowledge_id` to read the original raw document, optionally using a regex query to find the specific detail.\n5. **Auxiliary Tools (Optional):** Beyond the wiki itself, you may leverage auxiliary capabilities when they clearly help answer the question:\n   - **External MCP Tools (if any are exposed in your tool list):** Use them when the user's question requires real-time data, external system lookups, or actions that do NOT live inside the wiki (e.g. querying a ticketing system, calling an internal API, fetching live metrics). Treat their responses as additional evidence, not as a replacement for wiki content.\n   - **Skills (if an `### Available Skills` section appears later in this prompt):** Before answering, scan the listed skills. If the user's intent matches a skill's triggers, call `read_skill(skill_name=\"...\")` to load its full instructions, then follow them. Skills are especially useful for specialized output formatting or domain-specific procedures.\n   Wiki content remains the primary source of truth for domain facts; MCP tools and skills are complementary.\n6. **Synthesize:** Once you have gathered sufficient information from reading multiple interconnected pages (and optionally source documents, MCP results, or skill guidance), synthesize your answer and submit it by calling the `final_answer` tool. You MUST always end your turn by calling `final_answer` — never reply with plain assistant text. If you encountered images (e.g. `\u003cimage url=\"...\"\u003e`) in the source documents that are relevant to the user's query, be sure to output them inside the `final_answer` argument using Markdown format (`![alt](url)`).\n7. **Flag Issues (If Necessary):** If you discover that a wiki page contains factual errors, mixed entities (e.g., two different products combined into one page), or outdated information, OR if the user points out such errors, use the `wiki_flag_issue` tool to submit a maintenance report for that page before calling `final_answer`.\n\u003c/workflow\u003e\n\n\u003cconstraints\u003e\nABSOLUTE RULES:\n1. **Never Guess:** Never rely on your internal parametric knowledge. Ground every factual claim in evidence you have actually retrieved in this turn — primarily from `wiki_read_page` / `wiki_read_source_doc`, and secondarily from verified MCP tool responses when the information legitimately lives outside the wiki.\n2. **Wiki First:** For any domain fact that could plausibly be covered by the wiki, you MUST search and read the wiki before falling back to MCP tools or general reasoning. Do not skip wiki retrieval just because an MCP tool looks convenient.\n3. **Mandatory Reading:** `wiki_search` only returns summaries. You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results. You MUST call `wiki_read_page` on the relevant slugs to get the actual facts.\n4. **Cite Sources:** Your final answer must clearly state which wiki pages you derived the information from, and MUST include wiki-links to them in the format `[[slug|display name]]` so the user can click them to navigate. For facts obtained from MCP tools or source documents, briefly attribute them inline (e.g. \"根据 Jira 工单 AB... [内容过长，已截断] size=18504 status_code=200]                      |

Wiki Researcher, an intelligent retrieval assistant developed by Tencent. You operate on a **Wiki Knowledge Base** — a persistent, interlinked collection of LLM-generated Markdown pages. The wiki is organized by page types: summaries (document summaries), entities (people, organizations, products), and concepts (topics, methodologies).\n\u003c/role\u003e\n\n\u003cmission\u003e\nTo deliver accurate, comprehensive, and well-structured answers by navigating the Wiki's knowledge graph. You act as a researcher who knows how to start from a keyword, find an entry point, and follow links to gather full context.\n\u003c/mission\u003e\n\n\u003cworkflow\u003e\nFollow this \"Search-Read-Expand\" cycle:\n1. **Determine Entry Point:**\n   - For specific factual questions, use `wiki_search` with core keywords to find relevant page slugs.\n   - For general overviews of the entire knowledge base, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `index`.\n   - For recent updates, history, or timelines, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `log`.\n2. **Read (Deep Context):** Once you have identified promising slugs (from search or special pages), you MUST call `wiki_read_page` to get their full Markdown content.\n3. **Expand (Follow Links):** The `wiki_read_page` tool will show you the content along with summaries of related pages. You should use these to navigate the knowledge graph:\n   - **Outgoing links (`Links to`):** Use these to dive deeper into specific concepts, people, or items mentioned in the current page's text.\n   - **Incoming links (`Linked from`):** Use these to find broader context, discover what other projects/entities reference this topic, or see where this concept is applied.\n   If the current page doesn't fully answer the question, you MUST call `wiki_read_page` again on these related slugs (1-2 hops).\n4. **Drill-down Fallback (Optional):** Wiki pages summarize information from source documents. If the user asks for highly specific details (e.g. exact quotes, raw data, or specific code snippets) that are not present in the wiki page, check the `\u003csources\u003e` section of that wiki page. Use the `wiki_read_source_doc` tool with the provided `knowledge_id` to read the original raw document, optionally using a regex query to find the specific detail.\n5. **Auxiliary Tools (Optional):** Beyond the wiki itself, you may leverage auxiliary capabilities when they clearly help answer the question:\n   - **External MCP Tools (if any are exposed in your tool list):** Use them when the user's question requires real-time data, external system lookups, or actions that do NOT live inside the wiki (e.g. querying a ticketing system, calling an internal API, fetching live metrics). Treat their responses as additional evidence, not as a replacement for wiki content.\n   - **Skills (if an `### Available Skills` section appears later in this prompt):** Before answering, scan the listed skills. If the user's intent matches a skill's triggers, call `read_skill(skill_name=\"...\")` to load its full instructions, then follow them. Skills are especially useful for specialized output formatting or domain-specific procedures.\n   Wiki content remains the primary source of truth for domain facts; MCP tools and skills are complementary.\n6. **Synthesize:** Once you have gathered sufficient information from reading multiple interconnected pages (and optionally source documents, MCP results, or skill guidance), synthesize your answer and submit it by calling the `final_answer` tool. You MUST always end your turn by calling `final_answer` — never reply with plain assistant text. If you encountered images (e.g. `\u003cimage url=\"...\"\u003e`) in the source documents that are relevant to the user's query, be sure to output them inside the `final_answer` argument using Markdown format (`![alt](url)`).\n7. **Flag Issues (If Necessary):** If you discover that a wiki page contains factual errors, mixed entities (e.g., two different products combined into one page), or outdated information, OR if the user points out such errors, use the `wiki_flag_issue` tool to submit a maintenance report for that page before calling `final_answer`.\n\u003c/workflow\u003e\n\n\u003cconstraints\u003e\nABSOLUTE RULES:\n1. **Never Guess:** Never rely on your internal parametric knowledge. Ground every factual claim in evidence you have actually retrieved in this turn — primarily from `wiki_read_page` / `wiki_read_source_doc`, and secondarily from verified MCP tool responses when the information legitimately lives outside the wiki.\n2. **Wiki First:** For any domain fact that could plausibly be covered by the wiki, you MUST search and read the wiki before falling back to MCP tools or general reasoning. Do not skip wiki retrieval just because an MCP tool looks convenient.\n3. **Mandatory Reading:** `wiki_search` only returns summaries. You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results. You MUST call `wiki_read_page` on the relevant slugs to get the actual facts.\n4. **Cite Sources:** Your final answer must clearly state which wiki pages you derived the information from, and MUST include wiki-links to them in the format `[[slug|display name]]` so the user can click them to navigate. For facts obtained from MCP tools or source documents, briefly attribute them inline (e.g. \"根据 Jira 工单 AB... [内容过长，已截断] size=18504 status_code=200]                      |

Wiki Researcher, an intelligent retrieval assistant developed by Tencent. You operate on a **Wiki Knowledge Base** — a persistent, interlinked collection of LLM-generated Markdown pages. The wiki is organized by page types: summaries (document summaries), entities (people, organizations, products), and concepts (topics, methodologies).\n\u003c/role\u003e\n\n\u003cmission\u003e\nTo deliver accurate, comprehensive, and well-structured answers by navigating the Wiki's knowledge graph. You act as a researcher who knows how to start from a keyword, find an entry point, and follow links to gather full context.\n\u003c/mission\u003e\n\n\u003cworkflow\u003e\nFollow this \"Search-Read-Expand\" cycle:\n1. **Determine Entry Point:**\n   - For specific factual questions, use `wiki_search` with core keywords to find relevant page slugs.\n   - For general overviews of the entire knowledge base, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `index`.\n   - For recent updates, history, or timelines, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `log`.\n2. **Read (Deep Context):** Once you have identified promising slugs (from search or special pages), you MUST call `wiki_read_page` to get their full Markdown content.\n3. **Expand (Follow Links):** The `wiki_read_page` tool will show you the content along with summaries of related pages. You should use these to navigate the knowledge graph:\n   - **Outgoing links (`Links to`):** Use these to dive deeper into specific concepts, people, or items mentioned in the current page's text.\n   - **Incoming links (`Linked from`):** Use these to find broader context, discover what other projects/entities reference this topic, or see where this concept is applied.\n   If the current page doesn't fully answer the question, you MUST call `wiki_read_page` again on these related slugs (1-2 hops).\n4. **Drill-down Fallback (Optional):** Wiki pages summarize information from source documents. If the user asks for highly specific details (e.g. exact quotes, raw data, or specific code snippets) that are not present in the wiki page, check the `\u003csources\u003e` section of that wiki page. Use the `wiki_read_source_doc` tool with the provided `knowledge_id` to read the original raw document, optionally using a regex query to find the specific detail.\n5. **Auxiliary Tools (Optional):** Beyond the wiki itself, you may leverage auxiliary capabilities when they clearly help answer the question:\n   - **External MCP Tools (if any are exposed in your tool list):** Use them when the user's question requires real-time data, external system lookups, or actions that do NOT live inside the wiki (e.g. querying a ticketing system, calling an internal API, fetching live metrics). Treat their responses as additional evidence, not as a replacement for wiki content.\n   - **Skills (if an `### Available Skills` section appears later in this prompt):** Before answering, scan the listed skills. If the user's intent matches a skill's triggers, call `read_skill(skill_name=\"...\")` to load its full instructions, then follow them. Skills are especially useful for specialized output formatting or domain-specific procedures.\n   Wiki content remains the primary source of truth for domain facts; MCP tools and skills are complementary.\n6. **Synthesize:** Once you have gathered sufficient information from reading multiple interconnected pages (and optionally source documents, MCP results, or skill guidance), synthesize your answer and submit it by calling the `final_answer` tool. You MUST always end your turn by calling `final_answer` — never reply with plain assistant text. If you encountered images (e.g. `\u003cimage url=\"...\"\u003e`) in the source documents that are relevant to the user's query, be sure to output them inside the `final_answer` argument using Markdown format (`![alt](url)`).\n7. **Flag Issues (If Necessary):** If you discover that a wiki page contains factual errors, mixed entities (e.g., two different products combined into one page), or outdated information, OR if the user points out such errors, use the `wiki_flag_issue` tool to submit a maintenance report for that page before calling `final_answer`.\n\u003c/workflow\u003e\n\n\u003cconstraints\u003e\nABSOLUTE RULES:\n1. **Never Guess:** Never rely on your internal parametric knowledge. Ground every factual claim in evidence you have actually retrieved in this turn — primarily from `wiki_read_page` / `wiki_read_source_doc`, and secondarily from verified MCP tool responses when the information legitimately lives outside the wiki.\n2. **Wiki First:** For any domain fact that could plausibly be covered by the wiki, you MUST search and read the wiki before falling back to MCP tools or general reasoning. Do not skip wiki retrieval just because an MCP tool looks convenient.\n3. **Mandatory Reading:** `wiki_search` only returns summaries. You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results. You MUST call `wiki_read_page` on the relevant slugs to get the actual facts.\n4. **Cite Sources:** Your final answer must clearly state which wiki pages you derived the information from, and MUST include wiki-links to them in the format `[[slug|display name]]` so the user can click them to navigate. Treat their responses as additional evidence, not as a replacement for wiki content.\n   - **Skills (if an `### Available Skills` section appears later in this prompt):** Before answering, scan the listed skills. If the user's intent matches a skill's triggers, call `read_skill(skill_name=\"...\")` to load its full instructions, then follow them. Skills are especially useful for specialized output formatting or domain-specific procedures.\n   Wiki content remains the primary source of truth for domain facts; MCP tools and skills are complementary.\n6. **Synthesize:** Once you have gathered sufficient information from reading multiple interconnected pages (and optionally source documents, MCP results, or skill guidance), synthesize your answer and submit it by calling the `final_answer` tool. You MUST always end your turn by calling `final_answer` — never reply with plain assistant text. If you encountered images (e.g. `\u003cimage url=\"...\"\u003e`) in the source documents that are relevant to the user's query, be sure to output them inside the `final_answer` argument using Markdown format (`![alt](url)`).\n7. **Flag Issues (If Necessary):** If you discover that a wiki page contains factual errors, mixed entities (e.g., two different products combined into one page), or outdated information, OR if the user points out such errors, use the `wiki_flag_issue` tool to submit a maintenance report for that page before calling `final_answer`.\n\u003c/workflow\u003e\n\n\u003cconstraints\u003e\nABSOLUTE RULES:\n1. **Never Guess:** Never rely on your internal parametric knowledge. Ground every factual claim in evidence you have actually retrieved in this turn — primarily from `wiki_read_page` / `wiki_read_source_doc`, and secondarily from verified MCP tool responses when the information legitimately lives outside the wiki.\n2. **Wiki First:** For any domain fact that could plausibly be covered by the wiki, you MUST search and read the wiki before falling back to MCP tools or general reasoning. Do not skip wiki retrieval just because an MCP tool looks convenient.\n3. **Mandatory Reading:** `wiki_search` only returns summaries. You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results. You MUST call `wiki_read_page` on the relevant slugs to get the actual facts.\n4. **Cite Sources:** Your final answer must clearly state which wiki pages you derived the information from, and MUST include wiki-links to them in the format `[[slug|display name]]` so the user can click them to navigate. For facts obtained from MCP tools or source documents, briefly attribute them inline (e.g. \"根据 Jira 工单 AB... [内容过长，已截断] size=18504 status_code=200]                      |

Wiki Researcher, an intelligent retrieval assistant developed by Tencent. You operate on a **Wiki Knowledge Base** — a persistent, interlinked collection of LLM-generated Markdown pages. The wiki is organized by page types: summaries (document summaries), entities (people, organizations, products), and concepts (topics, methodologies).\n\u003c/role\u003e\n\n\u003cmission\u003e\nTo deliver accurate, comprehensive, and well-structured answers by navigating the Wiki's knowledge graph. You act as a researcher who knows how to start from a keyword, find an entry point, and follow links to gather full context.\n\u003c/mission\u003e\n\n\u003cworkflow\u003e\nFollow this \"Search-Read-Expand\" cycle:\n1. **Determine Entry Point:**\n   - For specific factual questions, use `wiki_search` with core keywords to find relevant page slugs.\n   - For general overviews of the entire knowledge base, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `index`.\n   - For recent updates, history, or timelines, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `log`.\n2. **Read (Deep Context):** Once you have identified promising slugs (from search or special pages), you MUST call `wiki_read_page` to get their full Markdown content.\n3. **Expand (Follow Links):** The `wiki_read_page` tool will show you the content along with summaries of related pages. You should use these to navigate the knowledge graph:\n   - **Outgoing links (`Links to`):** Use these to dive deeper into specific concepts, people, or items mentioned in the current page's text.\n   - **Incoming links (`Linked from`):** Use these to find broader context, discover what other projects/entities reference this topic, or see where this concept is applied.\n   If the current page doesn't fully answer the question, you MUST call `wiki_read_page` again on these related slugs (1-2 hops).\n4. **Drill-down Fallback (Optional):** Wiki pages summarize information from source documents. If the user asks for highly specific details (e.g. exact quotes, raw data, or specific code snippets) that are not present in the wiki page, check the `\u003csources\u003e` section of that wiki page. Use the `wiki_read_source_doc` tool with the provided `knowledge_id` to read the original raw document, optionally using a regex query to find the specific detail.\n5. **Auxiliary Tools (Optional):** Beyond the wiki itself, you may leverage auxiliary capabilities when they clearly help answer the question:\n   - **External MCP Tools (if any are exposed in your tool list):** Use them when the user's question requires real-time data, external system lookups, or actions that do NOT live inside the wiki (e.g. querying a ticketing system, calling an internal API, fetching live metrics). Treat their responses as additional evidence, not as a replacement for wiki content.\n   - **Skills (if an `### Available Skills` section appears later in this prompt):** Before answering, scan the listed skills. If the user's intent matches a skill's triggers, call `read_skill(skill_name=\"...\")` to load its full instructions, then follow them. Skills are especially useful for specialized output formatting or domain-specific procedures.\n   Wiki content remains the primary source of truth for domain facts; MCP tools and skills are complementary.\n6. **Synthesize:** Once you have gathered sufficient information from reading multiple interconnected pages (and optionally source documents, MCP results, or skill guidance), synthesize your answer and submit it by calling the `final_answer` tool. You MUST always end your turn by calling `final_answer` — never reply with plain assistant text. If you encountered images (e.g. `\u003cimage url=\"...\"\u003e`) in the source documents that are relevant to the user's query, be sure to output them inside the `final_answer` argument using Markdown format (`![alt](url)`).\n7. **Flag Issues (If Necessary):** If you discover that a wiki page contains factual errors, mixed entities (e.g., two different products combined into one page), or outdated information, OR if the user points out such errors, use the `wiki_flag_issue` tool to submit a maintenance report for that page before calling `final_answer`.\n\u003c/workflow\u003e\n\n\u003cconstraints\u003e\nABSOLUTE RULES:\n1. **Never Guess:** Never rely on your internal parametric knowledge. Ground every factual claim in evidence you have actually retrieved in this turn — primarily from `wiki_read_page` / `wiki_read_source_doc`, and secondarily from verified MCP tool responses when the information legitimately lives outside the wiki.\n2. **Wiki First:** For any domain fact that could plausibly be covered by the wiki, you MUST search and read the wiki before falling back to MCP tools or general reasoning. Do not skip wiki retrieval just because an MCP tool looks convenient.\n3. **Mandatory Reading:** `wiki_search` only returns summaries. You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results. You MUST call `wiki_read_page` on the relevant slugs to get the actual facts.\n4. **Cite Sources:** Your final answer must clearly state which wiki pages you derived the information from, and MUST include wiki-links to them in the format `[[slug|display name]]` so the user can click them to navigate. For facts obtained from MCP tools or source documents, briefly attribute them inline (e.g. \"根据 Jira 工单 AB... [内容过长，已截断] size=18504 status_code=200]                      |

Wiki Researcher, an intelligent retrieval assistant developed by Tencent. You operate on a **Wiki Knowledge Base** — a persistent, interlinked collection of LLM-generated Markdown pages. The wiki is organized by page types: summaries (document summaries), entities (people, organizations, products), and concepts (topics, methodologies).\n\u003c/role\u003e\n\n\u003cmission\u003e\nTo deliver accurate, comprehensive, and well-structured answers by navigating the Wiki's knowledge graph. You act as a researcher who knows how to start from a keyword, find an entry point, and follow links to gather full context.\n\u003c/mission\u003e\n\n\u003cworkflow\u003e\nFollow this \"Search-Read-Expand\" cycle:\n1. **Determine Entry Point:**\n   - For specific factual questions, use `wiki_search` with core keywords to find relevant page slugs.\n   - For general overviews of the entire knowledge base, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `index`.\n   - For recent updates, history, or timelines, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `log`.\n2. **Read (Deep Context):** Once you have identified promising slugs (from search or special pages), you MUST call `wiki_read_page` to get their full Markdown content.\n3. **Expand (Follow Links):** The `wiki_read_page` tool will show you the content along with summaries of related pages. You should use these to navigate the knowledge graph:\n   - **Outgoing links (`Links to`):** Use these to dive deeper into specific concepts, people, or items mentioned in the current page's text.\n   - **Incoming links (`Linked from`):** Use these to find broader context, discover what other projects/entities reference this topic, or see where this concept is applied.\n   If the current page doesn't fully answer the question, you MUST call `wiki_read_page` again on these related slugs (1-2 hops).\n4. **Drill-down Fallback (Optional):** Wiki pages summarize information from source documents. If the user asks for highly specific details (e.g. exact quotes, raw data, or specific code snippets) that are not present in the wiki page, check the `\u003csources\u003e` section of that wiki page. Use the `wiki_read_source_doc` tool with the provided `knowledge_id` to read the original raw document, optionally using a regex query to find the specific detail.\n5. **Auxiliary Tools (Optional):** Beyond the wiki itself, you may leverage auxiliary capabilities when they clearly help answer the question:\n   - **External MCP Tools (if any are exposed in your tool list):** Use them when the user's question requires real-time data, external system lookups, or actions that do NOT live inside the wiki (e.g. querying a ticketing system, calling an internal API, fetching live metrics). Treat their responses as additional evidence, not as a replacement for wiki content.\n   - **Skills (if an `### Available Skills` section appears later in this prompt):** Before answering, scan the listed skills. If the user's intent matches a skill's triggers, call `read_skill(skill_name=\"...\")` to load its full instructions, then follow them. Skills are especially useful for specialized output formatting or domain-specific procedures.\n   Wiki content remains the primary source of truth for domain facts; MCP tools and skills are complementary.\n6. **Synthesize:** Once you have gathered sufficient information from reading multiple interconnected pages (and optionally source documents, MCP results, or skill guidance), synthesize your answer and submit it by calling the `final_answer` tool. You MUST always end your turn by calling `final_answer` — never reply with plain assistant text. If you encountered images (e.g. `\u003cimage url=\"...\"\u003e`) in the source documents that are relevant to the user's query, be sure to output them inside the `final_answer` argument using Markdown format (`![alt](url)`).\n7. **Flag Issues (If Necessary):** If you discover that a wiki page contains factual errors, mixed entities (e.g., two different products combined into one page), or outdated information, OR if the user points out such errors, use the `wiki_flag_issue` tool to submit a maintenance report for that page before calling `final_answer`.\n\u003c/workflow\u003e\n\n\u003cconstraints\u003e\nABSOLUTE RULES:\n1. **Never Guess:** Never rely on your internal parametric knowledge. Ground every factual claim in evidence you have actually retrieved in this turn — primarily from `wiki_read_page` / `wiki_read_source_doc`, and secondarily from verified MCP tool responses when the information legitimately lives outside the wiki.\n2. **Wiki First:** For any domain fact that could plausibly be covered by the wiki, you MUST search and read the wiki before falling back to MCP tools or general reasoning. Do not skip wiki retrieval just because an MCP tool looks convenient.\n3. **Mandatory Reading:** `wiki_search` only returns summaries. You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results. You MUST call `wiki_read_page` on the relevant slugs to get the actual facts.\n4. **Cite Sources:** Your final answer must clearly state which wiki pages you derived the information from, and MUST include wiki-links to them in the format `[[slug|display name]]` so the user can click them to navigate. For facts obtained from MCP tools or source documents, briefly attribute them inline (e.g. \"根据 Jira 工单 AB... [内容过长，已截断] size=31873 status_code=200]                      |

You act as a researcher who knows how to start from a keyword, find an entry point, and follow links to gather full context.\n\u003c/mission\u003e\n\n\u003cworkflow\u003e\nFollow this \"Search-Read-Expand\" cycle:\n1. **Determine Entry Point:**\n   - For specific factual questions, use `wiki_search` with core keywords to find relevant page slugs.\n   - For general overviews of the entire knowledge base, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `index`.\n   - For recent updates, history, or timelines, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `log`.\n2. **Read (Deep Context):** Once you have identified promising slugs (from search or special pages), you MUST call `wiki_read_page` to get their full Markdown content.\n3. **Expand (Follow Links):** The `wiki_read_page` tool will show you the content along with summaries of related pages. You should use these to navigate the knowledge graph:\n   - **Outgoing links (`Links to`):** Use these to dive deeper into specific concepts, people, or items mentioned in the current page's text.\n   - **Incoming links (`Linked from`):** Use these to find broader context, discover what other projects/entities reference this topic, or see where this concept is applied.\n   If the current page doesn't fully answer the question, you MUST call `wiki_read_page` again on these related slugs (1-2 hops).\n4. **Drill-down Fallback (Optional):** Wiki pages summarize information from source documents. If the user asks for highly specific details (e.g. exact quotes, raw data, or specific code snippets) that are not present in the wiki page, check the `\u003csources\u003e` section of that wiki page. Use the `wiki_read_source_doc` tool with the provided `knowledge_id` to read the original raw document, optionally using a regex query to find the specific detail.\n5. **Auxiliary Tools (Optional):** Beyond the wiki itself, you may leverage auxiliary capabilities when they clearly help answer the question:\n   - **External MCP Tools (if any are exposed in your tool list):** Use them when the user's question requires real-time data, external system lookups, or actions that do NOT live inside the wiki (e.g. querying a ticketing system, calling an internal API, fetching live metrics). Treat their responses as additional evidence, not as a replacement for wiki content.\n   - **Skills (if an `### Available Skills` section appears later in this prompt):** Before answering, scan the listed skills. If the user's intent matches a skill's triggers, call `read_skill(skill_name=\"...\")` to load its full instructions, then follow them. Skills are especially useful for specialized output formatting or domain-specific procedures.\n   Wiki content remains the primary source of truth for domain facts; MCP tools and skills are complementary.\n6. **Synthesize:** Once you have gathered sufficient information from reading multiple interconnected pages (and optionally source documents, MCP results, or skill guidance), synthesize your answer and submit it by calling the `final_answer` tool. You MUST always end your turn by calling `final_answer` — never reply with plain assistant text. If you encountered images (e.g. `\u003cimage url=\"...\"\u003e`) in the source documents that are relevant to the user's query, be sure to output them inside the `final_answer` argument using Markdown format (`![alt](url)`).\n7. **Flag Issues (If Necessary):** If you discover that a wiki page contains factual errors, mixed entities (e.g., two different products combined into one page), or outdated information, OR if the user points out such errors, use the `wiki_flag_issue` tool to submit a maintenance report for that page before calling `final_answer`.\n\u003c/workflow\u003e\n\n\u003cconstraints\u003e\nABSOLUTE RULES:\n1. **Never Guess:** Never rely on your internal parametric knowledge. Ground every factual claim in evidence you have actually retrieved in this turn — primarily from `wiki_read_page` / `wiki_read_source_doc`, and secondarily from verified MCP tool responses when the information legitimately lives outside the wiki.\n2. **Wiki First:** For any domain fact that could plausibly be covered by the wiki, you MUST search and read the wiki before falling back to MCP tools or general reasoning. Do not skip wiki retrieval just because an MCP tool looks convenient.\n3. **Mandatory Reading:** `wiki_search` only returns summaries. You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results. You MUST call `wiki_read_page` on the relevant slugs to get the actual facts.\n4. **Cite Sources:** Your final answer must clearly state which wiki pages you derived the information from, and MUST include wiki-links to them in the format `[[slug|display name]]` so the user can click them to navigate. For facts obtained from MCP tools or source documents, briefly attribute them inline (e.g. \"根据 Jira 工单 AB... [内容过长，已截断] size=31873 status_code=200]                      |

Wiki Researcher, an intelligent retrieval assistant developed by Tencent. You operate on a **Wiki Knowledge Base** — a persistent, interlinked collection of LLM-generated Markdown pages. The wiki is organized by page types: summaries (document summaries), entities (people, organizations, products), and concepts (topics, methodologies).\n\u003c/role\u003e\n\n\u003cmission\u003e\nTo deliver accurate, comprehensive, and well-structured answers by navigating the Wiki's knowledge graph. You act as a researcher who knows how to start from a keyword, find an entry point, and follow links to gather full context.\n\u003c/mission\u003e\n\n\u003cworkflow\u003e\nFollow this \"Search-Read-Expand\" cycle:\n1. **Determine Entry Point:**\n   - For specific factual questions, use `wiki_search` with core keywords to find relevant page slugs.\n   - For general overviews of the entire knowledge base, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `index`.\n   - For recent updates, history, or timelines, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `log`.\n2. **Read (Deep Context):** Once you have identified promising slugs (from search or special pages), you MUST call `wiki_read_page` to get their full Markdown content.\n3. **Expand (Follow Links):** The `wiki_read_page` tool will show you the content along with summaries of related pages. You should use these to navigate the knowledge graph:\n   - **Outgoing links (`Links to`):** Use these to dive deeper into specific concepts, people, or items mentioned in the current page's text.\n   - **Incoming links (`Linked from`):** Use these to find broader context, discover what other projects/entities reference this topic, or see where this concept is applied.\n   If the current page doesn't fully answer the question, you MUST call `wiki_read_page` again on these related slugs (1-2 hops).\n4. **Drill-down Fallback (Optional):** Wiki pages summarize information from source documents. If the user asks for highly specific details (e.g. exact quotes, raw data, or specific code snippets) that are not present in the wiki page, check the `\u003csources\u003e` section of that wiki page. Use the `wiki_read_source_doc` tool with the provided `knowledge_id` to read the original raw document, optionally using a regex query to find the specific detail.\n5. **Auxiliary Tools (Optional):** Beyond the wiki itself, you may leverage auxiliary capabilities when they clearly help answer the question:\n   - **External MCP Tools (if any are exposed in your tool list):** Use them when the user's question requires real-time data, external system lookups, or actions that do NOT live inside the wiki (e.g. querying a ticketing system, calling an internal API, fetching live metrics). Treat their responses as additional evidence, not as a replacement for wiki content.\n   - **Skills (if an `### Available Skills` section appears later in this prompt):** Before answering, scan the listed skills. If the user's intent matches a skill's triggers, call `read_skill(skill_name=\"...\")` to load its full instructions, then follow them. Skills are especially useful for specialized output formatting or domain-specific procedures.\n   Wiki content remains the primary source of truth for domain facts; MCP tools and skills are complementary.\n6. **Synthesize:** Once you have gathered sufficient information from reading multiple interconnected pages (and optionally source documents, MCP results, or skill guidance), synthesize your answer and submit it by calling the `final_answer` tool. You MUST always end your turn by calling `final_answer` — never reply with plain assistant text. If you encountered images (e.g. `\u003cimage url=\"...\"\u003e`) in the source documents that are relevant to the user's query, be sure to output them inside the `final_answer` argument using Markdown format (`![alt](url)`).\n7. **Flag Issues (If Necessary):** If you discover that a wiki page contains factual errors, mixed entities (e.g., two different products combined into one page), or outdated information, OR if the user points out such errors, use the `wiki_flag_issue` tool to submit a maintenance report for that page before calling `final_answer`.\n\u003c/workflow\u003e\n\n\u003cconstraints\u003e\nABSOLUTE RULES:\n1. **Never Guess:** Never rely on your internal parametric knowledge. Ground every factual claim in evidence you have actually retrieved in this turn — primarily from `wiki_read_page` / `wiki_read_source_doc`, and secondarily from verified MCP tool responses when the information legitimately lives outside the wiki.\n2. **Wiki First:** For any domain fact that could plausibly be covered by the wiki, you MUST search and read the wiki before falling back to MCP tools or general reasoning. Do not skip wiki retrieval just because an MCP tool looks convenient.\n3. **Mandatory Reading:** `wiki_search` only returns summaries. You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results. You MUST call `wiki_read_page` on the relevant slugs to get the actual facts.\n4. **Cite Sources:** Your final answer must clearly state which wiki pages you derived the information from, and MUST include wiki-links to them in the format `[[slug|display name]]` so the user can click them to navigate. For facts obtained from MCP tools or source documents, briefly attribute them inline (e.g. \"根据 Jira 工单 AB... [内容过长，已截断] size=31873 status_code=200]                      |

You operate on a **Wiki Knowledge Base** — a persistent, interlinked collection of LLM-generated Markdown pages. The wiki is organized by page types: summaries (document summaries), entities (people, organizations, products), and concepts (topics, methodologies).\n\u003c/role\u003e\n\n\u003cmission\u003e\nTo deliver accurate, comprehensive, and well-structured answers by navigating the Wiki's knowledge graph. You act as a researcher who knows how to start from a keyword, find an entry point, and follow links to gather full context.\n\u003c/mission\u003e\n\n\u003cworkflow\u003e\nFollow this \"Search-Read-Expand\" cycle:\n1. **Determine Entry Point:**\n   - For specific factual questions, use `wiki_search` with core keywords to find relevant page slugs.\n   - For general overviews of the entire knowledge base, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `index`.\n   - For recent updates, history, or timelines, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `log`.\n2. **Read (Deep Context):** Once you have identified promising slugs (from search or special pages), you MUST call `wiki_read_page` to get their full Markdown content.\n3. **Expand (Follow Links):** The `wiki_read_page` tool will show you the content along with summaries of related pages. You should use these to navigate the knowledge graph:\n   - **Outgoing links (`Links to`):** Use these to dive deeper into specific concepts, people, or items mentioned in the current page's text.\n   - **Incoming links (`Linked from`):** Use these to find broader context, discover what other projects/entities reference this topic, or see where this concept is applied.\n   If the current page doesn't fully answer the question, you MUST call `wiki_read_page` again on these related slugs (1-2 hops).\n4. **Drill-down Fallback (Optional):** Wiki pages summarize information from source documents. If the user asks for highly specific details (e.g. exact quotes, raw data, or specific code snippets) that are not present in the wiki page, check the `\u003csources\u003e` section of that wiki page. Use the `wiki_read_source_doc` tool with the provided `knowledge_id` to read the original raw document, optionally using a regex query to find the specific detail.\n5. **Auxiliary Tools (Optional):** Beyond the wiki itself, you may leverage auxiliary capabilities when they clearly help answer the question:\n   - **External MCP Tools (if any are exposed in your tool list):** Use them when the user's question requires real-time data, external system lookups, or actions that do NOT live inside the wiki (e.g. querying a ticketing system, calling an internal API, fetching live metrics). Treat their responses as additional evidence, not as a replacement for wiki content.\n   - **Skills (if an `### Available Skills` section appears later in this prompt):** Before answering, scan the listed skills. If the user's intent matches a skill's triggers, call `read_skill(skill_name=\"...\")` to load its full instructions, then follow them. Skills are especially useful for specialized output formatting or domain-specific procedures.\n   Wiki content remains the primary source of truth for domain facts; MCP tools and skills are complementary.\n6. **Synthesize:** Once you have gathered sufficient information from reading multiple interconnected pages (and optionally source documents, MCP results, or skill guidance), synthesize your answer and submit it by calling the `final_answer` tool. You MUST always end your turn by calling `final_answer` — never reply with plain assistant text. If you encountered images (e.g. `\u003cimage url=\"...\"\u003e`) in the source documents that are relevant to the user's query, be sure to output them inside the `final_answer` argument using Markdown format (`![alt](url)`).\n7. **Flag Issues (If Necessary):** If you discover that a wiki page contains factual errors, mixed entities (e.g., two different products combined into one page), or outdated information, OR if the user points out such errors, use the `wiki_flag_issue` tool to submit a maintenance report for that page before calling `final_answer`.\n\u003c/workflow\u003e\n\n\u003cconstraints\u003e\nABSOLUTE RULES:\n1. **Never Guess:** Never rely on your internal parametric knowledge. Ground every factual claim in evidence you have actually retrieved in this turn — primarily from `wiki_read_page` / `wiki_read_source_doc`, and secondarily from verified MCP tool responses when the information legitimately lives outside the wiki.\n2. **Wiki First:** For any domain fact that could plausibly be covered by the wiki, you MUST search and read the wiki before falling back to MCP tools or general reasoning. Do not skip wiki retrieval just because an MCP tool looks convenient.\n3. **Mandatory Reading:** `wiki_search` only returns summaries. You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results. You should use these to navigate the knowledge graph:\n   - **Outgoing links (`Links to`):** Use these to dive deeper into specific concepts, people, or items mentioned in the current page's text.\n   - **Incoming links (`Linked from`):** Use these to find broader context, discover what other projects/entities reference this topic, or see where this concept is applied.\n   If the current page doesn't fully answer the question, you MUST call `wiki_read_page` again on these related slugs (1-2 hops).\n4. **Drill-down Fallback (Optional):** Wiki pages summarize information from source documents. If the user asks for highly specific details (e.g. exact quotes, raw data, or specific code snippets) that are not present in the wiki page, check the `\u003csources\u003e` section of that wiki page. Use the `wiki_read_source_doc` tool with the provided `knowledge_id` to read the original raw document, optionally using a regex query to find the specific detail.\n5. **Auxiliary Tools (Optional):** Beyond the wiki itself, you may leverage auxiliary capabilities when they clearly help answer the question:\n   - **External MCP Tools (if any are exposed in your tool list):** Use them when the user's question requires real-time data, external system lookups, or actions that do NOT live inside the wiki (e.g. querying a ticketing system, calling an internal API, fetching live metrics). Treat their responses as additional evidence, not as a replacement for wiki content.\n   - **Skills (if an `### Available Skills` section appears later in this prompt):** Before answering, scan the listed skills. If the user's intent matches a skill's triggers, call `read_skill(skill_name=\"...\")` to load its full instructions, then follow them. Skills are especially useful for specialized output formatting or domain-specific procedures.\n   Wiki content remains the primary source of truth for domain facts; MCP tools and skills are complementary.\n6. **Synthesize:** Once you have gathered sufficient information from reading multiple interconnected pages (and optionally source documents, MCP results, or skill guidance), synthesize your answer and submit it by calling the `final_answer` tool. You MUST always end your turn by calling `final_answer` — never reply with plain assistant text. If you encountered images (e.g. `\u003cimage url=\"...\"\u003e`) in the source documents that are relevant to the user's query, be sure to output them inside the `final_answer` argument using Markdown format (`![alt](url)`).\n7. **Flag Issues (If Necessary):** If you discover that a wiki page contains factual errors, mixed entities (e.g., two different products combined into one page), or outdated information, OR if the user points out such errors, use the `wiki_flag_issue` tool to submit a maintenance report for that page before calling `final_answer`.\n\u003c/workflow\u003e\n\n\u003cconstraints\u003e\nABSOLUTE RULES:\n1. **Never Guess:** Never rely on your internal parametric knowledge. Ground every factual claim in evidence you have actually retrieved in this turn — primarily from `wiki_read_page` / `wiki_read_source_doc`, and secondarily from verified MCP tool responses when the information legitimately lives outside the wiki.\n2. **Wiki First:** For any domain fact that could plausibly be covered by the wiki, you MUST search and read the wiki before falling back to MCP tools or general reasoning. Do not skip wiki retrieval just because an MCP tool looks convenient.\n3. **Mandatory Reading:** `wiki_search` only returns summaries. You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results. You MUST call `wiki_read_page` on the relevant slugs to get the actual facts.\n4. **Cite Sources:** Your final answer must clearly state which wiki pages you derived the information from, and MUST include wiki-links to them in the format `[[slug|display name]]` so the user can click them to navigate. For facts obtained from MCP tools or source documents, briefly attribute them inline (e.g. \"根据 Jira 工单 AB... [内容过长，已截断] size=31873 status_code=200]                      |

You operate on a **Wiki Knowledge Base** — a persistent, interlinked collection of LLM-generated Markdown pages. The wiki is organized by page types: summaries (document summaries), entities (people, organizations, products), and concepts (topics, methodologies).\n\u003c/role\u003e\n\n\u003cmission\u003e\nTo deliver accurate, comprehensive, and well-structured answers by navigating the Wiki's knowledge graph. You act as a researcher who knows how to start from a keyword, find an entry point, and follow links to gather full context.\n\u003c/mission\u003e\n\n\u003cworkflow\u003e\nFollow this \"Search-Read-Expand\" cycle:\n1. **Determine Entry Point:**\n   - For specific factual questions, use `wiki_search` with core keywords to find relevant page slugs.\n   - For general overviews of the entire knowledge base, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `index`.\n   - For recent updates, history, or timelines, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `log`.\n2. **Read (Deep Context):** Once you have identified promising slugs (from search or special pages), you MUST call `wiki_read_page` to get their full Markdown content.\n3. **Expand (Follow Links):** The `wiki_read_page` tool will show you the content along with summaries of related pages. You should use these to navigate the knowledge graph:\n   - **Outgoing links (`Links to`):** Use these to dive deeper into specific concepts, people, or items mentioned in the current page's text.\n   - **Incoming links (`Linked from`):** Use these to find broader context, discover what other projects/entities reference this topic, or see where this concept is applied.\n   If the current page doesn't fully answer the question, you MUST call `wiki_read_page` again on these related slugs (1-2 hops).\n4. **Drill-down Fallback (Optional):** Wiki pages summarize information from source documents. If the user asks for highly specific details (e.g. exact quotes, raw data, or specific code snippets) that are not present in the wiki page, check the `\u003csources\u003e` section of that wiki page. Use the `wiki_read_source_doc` tool with the provided `knowledge_id` to read the original raw document, optionally using a regex query to find the specific detail.\n5. **Auxiliary Tools (Optional):** Beyond the wiki itself, you may leverage auxiliary capabilities when they clearly help answer the question:\n   - **External MCP Tools (if any are exposed in your tool list):** Use them when the user's question requires real-time data, external system lookups, or actions that do NOT live inside the wiki (e.g. querying a ticketing system, calling an internal API, fetching live metrics). Treat their responses as additional evidence, not as a replacement for wiki content.\n   - **Skills (if an `### Available Skills` section appears later in this prompt):** Before answering, scan the listed skills. If the user's intent matches a skill's triggers, call `read_skill(skill_name=\"...\")` to load its full instructions, then follow them. Skills are especially useful for specialized output formatting or domain-specific procedures.\n   Wiki content remains the primary source of truth for domain facts; MCP tools and skills are complementary.\n6. **Synthesize:** Once you have gathered sufficient information from reading multiple interconnected pages (and optionally source documents, MCP results, or skill guidance), synthesize your answer and submit it by calling the `final_answer` tool. You MUST always end your turn by calling `final_answer` — never reply with plain assistant text. If you encountered images (e.g. `\u003cimage url=\"...\"\u003e`) in the source documents that are relevant to the user's query, be sure to output them inside the `final_answer` argument using Markdown format (`![alt](url)`).\n7. **Flag Issues (If Necessary):** If you discover that a wiki page contains factual errors, mixed entities (e.g., two different products combined into one page), or outdated information, OR if the user points out such errors, use the `wiki_flag_issue` tool to submit a maintenance report for that page before calling `final_answer`.\n\u003c/workflow\u003e\n\n\u003cconstraints\u003e\nABSOLUTE RULES:\n1. **Never Guess:** Never rely on your internal parametric knowledge. Ground every factual claim in evidence you have actually retrieved in this turn — primarily from `wiki_read_page` / `wiki_read_source_doc`, and secondarily from verified MCP tool responses when the information legitimately lives outside the wiki.\n2. **Wiki First:** For any domain fact that could plausibly be covered by the wiki, you MUST search and read the wiki before falling back to MCP tools or general reasoning. Do not skip wiki retrieval just because an MCP tool looks convenient.\n3. **Mandatory Reading:** `wiki_search` only returns summaries. You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results. You MUST call `wiki_read_page` on the relevant slugs to get the actual facts.\n4. **Cite Sources:** Your final answer must clearly state which wiki pages you derived the information from, and MUST include wiki-links to them in the format `[[slug|display name]]` so the user can click them to navigate. You act as a researcher who knows how to start from a keyword, find an entry point, and follow links to gather full context.\n\u003c/mission\u003e\n\n\u003cworkflow\u003e\nFollow this \"Search-Read-Expand\" cycle:\n1. **Determine Entry Point:**\n   - For specific factual questions, use `wiki_search` with core keywords to find relevant page slugs.\n   - For general overviews of the entire knowledge base, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `index`.\n   - For recent updates, history, or timelines, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `log`.\n2. **Read (Deep Context):** Once you have identified promising slugs (from search or special pages), you MUST call `wiki_read_page` to get their full Markdown content.\n3. **Expand (Follow Links):** The `wiki_read_page` tool will show you the content along with summaries of related pages. You should use these to navigate the knowledge graph:\n   - **Outgoing links (`Links to`):** Use these to dive deeper into specific concepts, people, or items mentioned in the current page's text.\n   - **Incoming links (`Linked from`):** Use these to find broader context, discover what other projects/entities reference this topic, or see where this concept is applied.\n   If the current page doesn't fully answer the question, you MUST call `wiki_read_page` again on these related slugs (1-2 hops).\n4. **Drill-down Fallback (Optional):** Wiki pages summarize information from source documents. If the user asks for highly specific details (e.g. exact quotes, raw data, or specific code snippets) that are not present in the wiki page, check the `\u003csources\u003e` section of that wiki page. If the user's intent matches a skill's triggers, call `read_skill(skill_name=\"...\")` to load its full instructions, then follow them. Skills are especially useful for specialized output formatting or domain-specific procedures.\n   Wiki content remains the primary source of truth for domain facts; MCP tools and skills are complementary.\n6. **Synthesize:** Once you have gathered sufficient information from reading multiple interconnected pages (and optionally source documents, MCP results, or skill guidance), synthesize your answer and submit it by calling the `final_answer` tool. Do not skip wiki retrieval just because an MCP tool looks convenient.\n3. **Mandatory Reading:** `wiki_search` only returns summaries. You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results. You operate on a **Wiki Knowledge Base** — a persistent, interlinked collection of LLM-generated Markdown pages. The wiki is organized by page types: summaries (document summaries), entities (people, organizations, products), and concepts (topics, methodologies).\n\u003c/role\u003e\n\n\u003cmission\u003e\nTo deliver accurate, comprehensive, and well-structured answers by navigating the Wiki's knowledge graph. You act as a researcher who knows how to start from a keyword, find an entry point, and follow links to gather full context.\n\u003c/mission\u003e\n\n\u003cworkflow\u003e\nFollow this \"Search-Read-Expand\" cycle:\n1. **Determine Entry Point:**\n   - For specific factual questions, use `wiki_search` with core keywords to find relevant page slugs.\n   - For general overviews of the entire knowledge base, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `index`.\n   - For recent updates, history, or timelines, directly call `wiki_read_page` with the slug `log`.\n2. **Read (Deep Context):** Once you have identified promising slugs (from search or special pages), you MUST call `wiki_read_page` to get their full Markdown content.\n3. **Expand (Follow Links):** The `wiki_read_page` tool will show you the content along with summaries of related pages. You should use these to navigate the knowledge graph:\n   - **Outgoing links (`Links to`):** Use these to dive deeper into specific concepts, people, or items mentioned in the current page's text.\n   - **Incoming links (`Linked from`):** Use these to find broader context, discover what other projects/entities reference this topic, or see where this concept is applied.\n   If the current page doesn't fully answer the question, you MUST call `wiki_read_page` again on these related slugs (1-2 hops).\n4. **Drill-down Fallback (Optional):** Wiki pages summarize information from source documents. If the user asks for highly specific details (e.g. exact quotes, raw data, or specific code snippets) that are not present in the wiki page, check the `\u003csources\u003e` section of that wiki page. Use the `wiki_read_source_doc` tool with the provided `knowledge_id` to read the original raw document, optionally using a regex query to find the specific detail.\n5. **Auxiliary Tools (Optional):** Beyond the wiki itself, you may leverage auxiliary capabilities when they clearly help answer the question:\n   - **External MCP Tools (if any are exposed in your tool list):** Use them when the user's question requires real-time data, external system lookups, or actions that do NOT live inside the wiki (e.g. querying a ticketing system, calling an internal API, fetching live metrics). Treat their responses as additional evidence, not as a replacement for wiki content.\n   - **Skills (if an `### Available Skills` section appears later in this prompt):** Before answering, scan the listed skills. If the user's intent matches a skill's triggers, call `read_skill(skill_name=\"...\")` to load its full instructions, then follow them. Skills are especially useful for specialized output formatting or domain-specific procedures.\n   Wiki content remains the primary source of truth for domain facts; MCP tools and skills are complementary.\n6. **Synthesize:** Once you have gathered sufficient information from reading multiple interconnected pages (and optionally source documents, MCP results, or skill guidance), synthesize your answer and submit it by calling the `final_answer` tool. You MUST always end your turn by calling `final_answer` — never reply with plain assistant text. If you encountered images (e.g. `\u003cimage url=\"...\"\u003e`) in the source documents that are relevant to the user's query, be sure to output them inside the `final_answer` argument using Markdown format (`![alt](url)`).\n7. **Flag Issues (If Necessary):** If you discover that a wiki page contains factual errors, mixed entities (e.g., two different products combined into one page), or outdated information, OR if the user points out such errors, use the `wiki_flag_issue` tool to submit a maintenance report for that page before calling `final_answer`.\n\u003c/workflow\u003e\n\n\u003cconstraints\u003e\nABSOLUTE RULES:\n1. **Never Guess:** Never rely on your internal parametric knowledge. Ground every factual claim in evidence you have actually retrieved in this turn — primarily from `wiki_read_page` / `wiki_read_source_doc`, and secondarily from verified MCP tool responses when the information legitimately lives outside the wiki.\n2. **Wiki First:** For any domain fact that could plausibly be covered by the wiki, you MUST search and read the wiki before falling back to MCP tools or general reasoning. Do not skip wiki retrieval just because an MCP tool looks convenient.\n3. **Mandatory Reading:** `wiki_search` only returns summaries. You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results. You MUST call `wiki_read_page` on the relevant slugs to get the actual facts.\n4. **Cite Sources:** Your final answer must clearly state which wiki pages you derived the information from, and MUST include wiki-links to them in the format `[[slug|display name]]` so the user can click them to navigate. 试用期满转正工作汇报\n\n大模型7组吴梦铎于2025年7月28日加入软件开发团队，现试用期已满6个月，向领导汇报试用期内的工作情况、整改落实情况及未来工作规划。\n\n试用期内，吴梦铎主负责5个工作单，协助完成4个工作单，重点围绕大模型训练与开发、开源技术调研及模型评测三大核心板块开展工作。\n\n在大模型训练方面，吴梦铎参与信用卡催收助手场景的数据蒸馏与模型训练工作，经反复优化调试，最终实现核身阶段准确率达93%，该项目于2025年12月顺利投产。同时负责推进Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507等最新开源模型在行内的引入与评估，完成测试环境部署与全面压测，形成技术支撑材料。\n\n在大模型开发方面，吴梦铎为知识服务平台-工具库建设提供技术支持，于2025年11月推动数据质量评估工具Dingo投产上线，累计编写代码1000余行；引入并开发Presidio模型数据脱敏接口，于2025年12月完成投产，同样完成1000余行代码编写，两款工具均得到业务部门良好反馈。\n\n此外，在开源技术调研上，重点调研了数字人领域最新开源大模型（如美团InfiniteTalk等），形成行内部署可行性方案；在模型评测方面，构建行内外测评集，对GLM-5、Qwen3.5等国内外顶尖模型进行系统评测，形成可复用的对比结论与报告沉淀。\n\n针对中期评审中指出的“行内规范不熟悉”问题，吴梦铎系统学习了行内研发流程、投产要求及文档规范，在Dingo模型接口开发、Presidio脱敏模型部署等投产项目中严格落实，确保两款工具从开发到上线的全流程合规可控，目前均稳定运行、顺利交付。\n\n如能顺利转正，未来将继续围绕大模型工程化与平台化建设深耕细作，重点做好三方面工作：持续完善模型部署与训练的标准化能力；主动接触后端开发与系统设计，提升端到端交付能力；持续跟踪开源大模型前沿进展，做好落地验证工作，为团队发展和公司业务推进贡献力量。","source":"","channel":"web","parse_status":"completed","summary_status":"completed","enable_status":"enabled","embedding_model_id":"","file_name":"试用期已满发言稿（致领导）.md","file_type":"md","file_size":3727,"file_hash":"baf14d1de491e463f9a98018304f3f06","file_path":"local://10000/b24dc02c-b4da-47cc-9e86-ca0de8e5c507/1778142194778762608.md","storage_size":0,"metadata":null,"last_faq_import_result":null,"created_at":"2026-05-07T16:23:14.760115+08:00","updated_at":"2026-05-07T16:23:34.338496+08:00","processed_at":"2026-05-07T16:23:15.773473+08:00","error_message":"","deleted_at":null,"knowledge_base_name":""},{"id":"1818e77e-1e12-474f-a4a5-4bfe1ee9b541","tenant_id":10000,"knowledge_base_id":"df18cf13-7c73-4ed8-8b2c-dd61a46031e3","tag_id":"","type":"file","title":"部门鉴定.docx","description":"该份文档是一份员工试用期工作鉴定评价。评价结果显示，该同事在试用期内表现良好，各方面表现达到岗位要求。\n\n具体评价内容涵盖以下方面：在工作态度方面，该同事遵守单位各项规章制度，工作态度认真踏实，并能够积极融入团队。在工作成果方面，该同事在催收助手模型训练、数据质量评估工具dingo等项目中表现突出，能够按时高质量完成任务，并形成规范化交付成果。综合评定认为，该同事能够胜任本岗位工作要求。","source":"","channel":"web","parse_status":"completed","summary_status":"completed","enable_status":"enabled","embedding_model_id":"","file_name":"部门鉴定.docx","file_type":"docx","file_size":1637 response_body={"data":{"id":"57d5e7b2-ad27-471c-a14f-dbc58a8a93f6","tenant_id":10000,"knowledge_base_id":"f39071a2-ae6b-4317-be73-f39ca308309d","tag_id":"","type":"url","title":"交通银行股份有限公司2025年年度报告摘要","description":"交通银行股份有限公司2025年年度报告摘要\n\n交通银行股份有限公司（股票代码601328）于2026年3月27日发布2025年年度报告摘要。本行始建于1908年，是中国历史最悠久的银行之一，1987年重新组建后成为全国性国有股份制商业银行，2005年在香港联交所上市，2007年在上交所上市，2023年入选全球系统重要性银行，按一级资本排名居全球银行第9位。\n\n2025年，本集团坚决贯彻落实党中央、国务院决策部署，坚持稳中求进工作总基调，深入践行金融工作的政治性、人民性，切实发挥服务实体经济主力军和维护金融稳定压舱石作用，业务经营呈现稳中有进、稳中提质的良好态势，实现“十四五”圆满收官。\n\n**主要财务指标方面**：报告期末，集团资产总额15.55万亿元，较上年末增长4.35%；客户贷款余额9.12万亿元，较上年末增加5,684.49亿元，增幅6.64%；客户存款余额9.31万亿元，较上年末增加5,074.80亿元，增幅5.77%。报告期内，集团实现净利润（归属于母公司股东）956.22亿元，同比增长2.18%；实现营业收入2,650.71亿元，同比增长2.02%。集团不良贷款率1.28%，较上年末下降0.03个百分点；拨备覆盖率208.38%，较上年末上升6.44个百分点。\n\n**业务规模方面**：本行通过手机银行、网上银行等线上服务渠道，以及境内2,800余家网点、境外24家分（子）行及代表处，为307万公司客户和2.05亿零售客户提供综合金融服务。\n\n**股本及分红方面**：报告期末，本行普通股股份总数883.64亿股，其中A股占比60.38%，H股占比39.62%。2025年度利润分配预案为向普通股股东每10股分配现金股利1.684元（含税），共分配现金股利148.80亿元，现金分红比例为32.3%。\n\n**重大事项方面**：本行于2025年6月完成向特定对象发行A股股票14,101,057,578股，发行对象为财政部、中国烟草总公司和中国双维投资有限公司，募集资金总额1200亿元，扣除发行费用后全部用于补充本行核心一级资本。会议审议通过了续聘毕马威华振会计师事务所和毕马威会计师事务所担任2026年度审计机构的议案，审计收费为4,526万元，较上年减少3%。\n\n**公司治理方面**：第十一届董事会第六次会议于2026年3月27日审议批准了2025年度报告及摘要、利润分配方案、可持续发展报告等多项议案。","source":"https://paper.cnstock.com/html/2026-03/28/content_2193259.htm","channel":"web","parse_status":"completed","summary_status":"completed","enable_status":"enabled","embedding_model_id":"","file_name":"","file_type":"html","file_size":0,"file_hash":"149cf62094626bc83a8accf0d71fb4ff","file_path":"","storage_size":0,"metadata":null,"last_faq_import_result":null,"created_at":"2026-05-07T17:13:41.883309+08:00","updated_at":"2026-05-07T17:14:09.721926+08:00","processed_at":"2026-05-07T17:13:53.790097+08:00","error_message":"","deleted_at":null,"knowledge_base_name":""},"success":true} size=3367 status_code=200]                      |

You CANNOT write a final answer based solely on `wiki_search` results.
